
> You are a senior AI/ML engineer and software architect.

  I need you to build a production-quality RAG Generator for an Agentic Coding
  Assessment.

  FIRST: Read the attached candidate brief carefully and treat it as the
  primary source of requirements.

  The core requirement is:

  - Accept documents at runtime
  - Create a RAG application over those documents
  - Allow users to ask questions and receive grounded answers
  - Work with different document sets without code changes
  - Architecture, stack, models, tools and approach are up to us

  The submission requires:
  1. A Git repository containing the working code
  2. Complete AI-agent transcripts
  3. Strong architectural reasoning and documentation

  ==================================================
  IMPORTANT WORKING PRINCIPLES
  ==================================================

  Do NOT jump directly into writing code.

  Treat this as a real engineering project.

  Before implementation:

  1. Analyze the requirements.
  2. Identify ambiguities and assumptions.
  3. Identify the major architectural decisions.
  4. Identify reasonable alternatives for each decision.
  5. Create a trade-off analysis.
  6. Select an architecture based on the assessment requirements.
  7. Explain why each component was selected.
  8. Define an implementation plan.
  9. Only then start implementing.

  The goal is NOT to build the most complicated RAG system.

  The goal is to build a clean, extensible, well-engineered RAG Generator and
  demonstrate strong engineering judgment.

  Prefer:
  - simplicity
  - modularity
  - testability
  - observability
  - reproducibility
  - configurable components
  - clear separation of concerns

  Avoid unnecessary complexity unless there is a demonstrated reason for it.

  ==================================================
  ARCHITECTURE PHILOSOPHY
  ==================================================

  Start with a strong baseline RAG architecture.

  Potential architecture:

  Documents
      ↓
  Document Loader / Parser
      ↓
  Text Extraction
      ↓
  Chunking
      ↓
  Embedding
      ↓
  Vector Store
      ↓
  Retriever
      ↓
  Optional Reranker
      ↓
  Context Construction
      ↓
  LLM
      ↓
  Grounded Answer + Citations

  If agentic behavior is useful, introduce it deliberately.

  For example:

  User Query
      ↓
  Retrieve
      ↓
  Evaluate Context
      ↓
  Enough relevant context?
      ├── YES → Generate Answer
      │
      └── NO → Rewrite Query
                    ↓
                 Retrieve Again
                    ↓
                 Generate Answer

  Do NOT add agents, query rewriting, reranking, hy
  ──── (596 lines hidden) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  02_ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md
  - 03_TRADEOFF_ANALYSIS.md
  - 04_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md

  STOP and review the design internally before coding.

  STEP 4
  Implement the project incrementally.

  After each major implementation phase:

  - run tests
  - inspect failures
  - update documentation
  - update DECISION_LOG.md if a decision changed

  STEP 5
  Create:
  - 05_API_AND_COMPONENT_DESIGN.md
  - 06_EVALUATION.md
  - 07_TESTING.md
  - 08_FAILURE_MODES.md
  - 09_SECURITY_AND_PRIVACY.md
  - 10_OBSERVABILITY.md

  STEP 6
  Run the complete test suite.

  STEP 7
  Perform a code-quality review.

  STEP 8
  Perform an architecture review.

  STEP 9
  Update README.md.

  STEP 10
  Perform a final assessment review.

  ==================================================
  FINAL SELF-REVIEW
  ==================================================

  Before considering the project complete, answer these questions:

  1. Can I upload a completely different document set without changing code?

  2. Can I replace the embedding provider without rewriting retrieval?

  3. Can I replace the vector store?

  4. Can I replace the LLM?

  5. Can the system explain where an answer came from?

  6. What happens when there is no answer in the documents?

  7. What happens when retrieval returns bad context?

  8. What happens when an API fails?

  9. How do I know whether retrieval is working?

  10. How do I know whether generated answers are grounded?

  11. What are the main architectural trade-offs?

  12. Why did I choose this architecture instead of the alternatives?

  13. Is every major dependency justified?

  14. Is the implementation simpler than it needs to be, or more complicated
  than it needs to be?

  15. Can an evaluator clone the repo and run it easily?

  ==================================================
  IMPORTANT OUTPUT FORMAT
  ==================================================

  As you work, clearly separate:

  [REQUIREMENT]
  [ASSUMPTION]
  [DESIGN DECISION]
  [TRADE-OFF]
  [IMPLEMENTATION]
  [TEST RESULT]
  [OPEN QUESTION]

  Do not silently make important architectural decisions.

  When you make a significant decision, explain it before implementing it.

  At the end, provide:

  1. Final project structure
  2. Final architecture
  3. Major decisions
  4. Major trade-offs
  5. How to run
  6. How to test
  7. Known limitations
  8. Future improvements
  9. Files created/modified
  10. Assessment-readiness checklist

  Remember: the evaluator should be able to see not only that the RAG
  application works, but that the architecture was deliberately designed and
  the trade-offs were understood.

● Moving on to storage design: I'll keep chunks and vectors together in one
  atomically-replaced index.npz to avoid misalignment from partial writes, with
  the catalog as a separate file that self-heals from crashes since ingestion
  always deletes-then-readds chunks and orphans are dropped on load.

● I'm treating both the relevance gate failure and the LLM's answerable=false as
  signals of insufficient context, so either one triggers the corrective
  rewrite retry when enabled—otherwise vocabulary mismatches would get gated out
  before rewriting could fix them. Now I'll update the query-flow diagram to
  reflect this.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Two surprises: dense retrieval beats hybrid (0.952 vs 0.905 hit@3, since BM25
  hurts paraphrase questions), and the cross-encoder reranker boosts hit@1 but
  adds 1.5s/query with little benefit since dense hit@5 is already 1.00. Before
  changing any defaults, I'll dig into exactly which questions fail.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The failure analysis shows dense retrieval beats hybrid because BM25 misses
  vocabulary mismatches like "recharge" vs "charging," and similarity scores
  separate cleanly only for off-topic queries. Based on this, I'm switching
  defaults to dense retrieval, chunk size 700/overlap 140, and a 0.50 relevance
  threshold, keeping the reranker off, while noting in the docs that 21
  questions only give directional evidence, not statistical significance.

  ⎿  Updated docs\DECISION_LOG.md (+75 -0)
       70    Set it to `false` for Bedrock, Vertex, or a proxy.
       71  
       72  <!-- Entries below were added during and after implementation.
            -->
       73 +                                                              
       74 +### 2026-09-30 — D7: Parsers take bytes, not paths            
       75 +- **Context:** HTTP uploads would otherwise have to be written
          + to temporary files before                                    
       76 +  parsing.                                                    
       77 +- **Options:** Temporary file on disk / parse from memory.    
       78 +- **Decision:** `DocumentParser.parse(data: bytes, source: str
          +)`. PyMuPDF                                                   
       79 +  (`stream=`) and python-docx (`BytesIO`) both support this.  
       80 +- **Reason:** Uploaded documents never touch disk except in th
          +e index itself. That                                          
       81 +  removes a whole class of temp-file cleanup and leakage issue
          +s (09_SECURITY).                                              
       82 +- **Trade-off:** The whole file is held in memory. Bounded by 
          +`RAG_MAX_FILE_MB`                                             
       83 +  (50 MB by default).                                         
       84 +                                                              
       85 +### 2026-09-30 — D8: Default retrieval mode revised from `hybr
          +id` to `dense` (evidence-driven)                              
       86 +- **Context:** ADR-005 initially defaulted to hybrid on the pr
          +ior that BM25 rescues                                         
       87 +  exact-identifier queries. The retrieval sweep (`scripts/retr
          +ieval_sweep.py`; 26                                           
       88 +  questions, 21 of them answerable, 6 documents) measured:    
       89 +                                                              
       90 +  | chunk | mode | hit@1 | hit@3 | hit@5 | MRR |              
       91 +  |---|---|---|---|---|---|                                   
       92 +  | 700 | dense | 0.905 | 0.952 | 1.000 | 0.933 |             
       93 +  | 700 | bm25 | 0.714 | 0.857 | 0.857 | 0.770 |              
       94 +  | 700 | hybrid | 0.810 | 0.905 | 0.905 | 0.854 |            
       95 +                                                              
       96 +  Exact-ID questions scored hit@3 = 1.00 in *every* mode, so B
          +M25 added nothing there.                                      
       97 +  Paraphrase questions were hurt: for q10 ("fully recharge"), 
          +BM25 has no match                                             
       98 +  because it doesn't stem ("recharge" vs "charging"). Under eq
          +ual-weight RRF, chunks                                        
       99 +  present in both lists then outrank a dense-only hit, pushing
          + the right chunk out of                                       
      100 +  the top 10.                                                 
      101 +- **Options:** Keep hybrid / switch to dense / weighted fusion
          + / add stemming to BM25.                                      
      102 +- **Decision:** Default `RAG_RETRIEVAL_MODE=dense`. Hybrid and
          + BM25 stay implemented                                        
      103 +  and selectable.                                             
      104 +- **Reason:** It's measured-better or equal on every category 
          +here, and it's the                                            
      105 +  simpler pipeline.                                           
      106 +- **Trade-off:** The sample is small (a one-question differenc
          +e at hit@3), so this                                          
      107 +  is directional, not significant. Corpora with many near-iden
          +tical identifiers (part                                       
      108 +  numbers, ticket IDs, legal clause numbers) are where BM25 sh
          +ould earn its place.                                          
      109 +  Switch to `hybrid` there and re-run `rag eval` to confirm.  
     … 38 more lines (ctrl+o to expand)

› Message from a2e1ebadfcf3780d4
  I found 15 issues and fixed nothing, since this was a read-only review. The
  test suite passes (127 tests). The vendor boundary is clean: `anthropic` is
  imported only in `generation/anthropic_provider.py`, which the factory imports
  lazily, and `fastembed`, `pymupdf` and `docx` stay inside their own adapters.
  Unless marked otherwise, each finding below was reproduced with a scratch
  script, confirmed by reading the code, or both. All paths are relative to
  `C:/Users/rigu/Documents/RAG_project/src/rag_generator/`.

  **1. A failed replace deletes the old version of the document** —
  `orchestration/ingestion_service.py:134-139`
  - **Defect:** The previous version with the same name is removed from the
  store and the catalog *before* `store.add()`. If `add()` then raises
  `IndexMismatchError` (a model or dimension mismatch, which is a `RAGError`),
  the old version is already gone.
  - **Scenario (reproduced):** A collection is built with `hashing:256` and
  holds `b.txt` and `d.txt`. With the config changed to 128 dimensions,
  uploading a new `b.txt` returns `failed IndexMismatchError`. The in-memory
  catalog and store now hold only `d.txt`, and the next `delete_document` or
  persist writes that loss to disk.
  - **Confidence:** confirmed.
  - **Fix:** Call `store._ensure_compatible()` and the dimension check before
  any mutation, or add the new version first and delete the old one only after
  `add` succeeds.

  **2. A crash during delete or replace leaves a "ghost" catalog record that can
  never be re-ingested** — `storage/collection.py:43-47`,
  `ingestion_service.py:121`
  - **Defect:** The index is persisted before the catalog. If the process dies
  after a delete or replace but before the catalog write, the catalog still
  lists the document while the index has no chunks for it.
  - **Consequence:** `drop_orphans` only handles the opposite case (chunks with
  no catalog entry). The duplicate check `catalog.get(doc_id)` then reports
  `skipped_duplicate` for every re-ingest.
  - **Scenario (reproduced):** After the simulated crash, the catalog is
  `['a.txt']` with 0 chunks, and re-ingesting `a.txt` gives `skipped_duplicate`
  with still 0 chunks. This contradicts `docs/01_ARCHITECTURE.md` §7
  ("re-ingesting the file restores it").
  - **Confidence:** confirmed.
  - **Fix:** On open, reconcile in both directions (also drop catalog records
  that have no chunks). Also make the duplicate check require that chunks
  actually exist.

  **3. Files with the same base name overwrite each other in one CLI ingest** —
  `ingestion_service.py:47`, `:73`
  - **Defect:** Explicitly named files use `path.name` as the source, and each
  walked directory uses paths relative to itself. So `a/notes.md` and
  `b/notes.md` share the source `notes.md`, and replace-by-name silently drops
  the first one.
  - **Scenario (reproduced):** `rag ingest a/notes.md b/notes.md` and `rag
  ingest a b` both report `ingested` then `replaced`, leaving 1 document.
  - **Confidence:** confirmed.
  - **Fix:** Use a collision-free source key (a path relative to a common root,
  or the resolved path). At minimum, reject duplicate source names within a
  batch.

  **4. The chunker silently drops short trailing text** —
  `chunking/recursive.py:36` together with `_merge`/`_overlap_tail` at `:72-95`
  - **Defect:** When the last piece does not fit and the previous piece is
  longer than the overlap, the tail is empty. The last piece becomes a chunk on
  its own, and the `min_chars` filter then discards it.
  - **Scenario (reproduced):** A 691-character paragraph followed by
  `"\n\nRefund: 14 days."` produces one chunk that does not contain "Refund".
  Closing lines such as "Contact: x@y.com" or "Total: $5,000" are unsearchable.
  - **Confidence:** confirmed.
  - **Fix:** In `_merge`, append a final piece shorter than `min_chars` to the
  previous chunk (allowing a small overflow). Apply `min_chars` only to whole
  sections.

  **5. Race in the BM25 cache can leave a stale index in place** —
  `storage/collection.py:34-40`
  - **Defect:** `derived()` reads `store.version` *after* `build()` runs. A
  query thread builds from the chunks as of version v, a concurrent ingest or
  delete moves the store to v+1, and the stale index is cached under v+1.
  - **Consequence:** BM25 and hybrid searches then miss new documents, or return
  and cite deleted ones, until the next mutation. The API runs sync endpoints
  in a threadpool, and readers do not take the lock.
  - **Confidence:** confirmed by reading; depends on timing.
  - **Fix:** Capture `v = self.store.version` before `build()` and store `(v,
  value)`.

  **6. One unexpected parser exception aborts the whole batch** —
  `ingestion_service.py:152`
  - **Defect:** Only `RAGError` and `OSError` are caught per file. The PDF
  parser wraps only `pymupdf.open()`, so a `RuntimeError` or `ValueError` from
  `page.get_text()` on a damaged page (or any library bug) escapes.
  - **Consequence:** The API returns a 500 and no report. Files already
  processed in the batch are live in memory but not persisted. This contradicts
  the documented per-file isolation.
  - **Confidence:** plausible.
  - **Fix:** In `_ingest_one`, catch `Exception`, log it, and return `failed`
  with an internal error type. Alternatively, wrap each parser's extraction
  loop.

  **7. A failed rewrite call turns a valid abstention into a 503** —
  `orchestration/query_service.py:126-128`
  - **Defect:** When the gate or the LLM has already decided to abstain, the
  rewrite call is optional, but any `GenerationError` from it (rate limit,
  timeout) propagates out of `ask()`.
  - **Scenario:** With `RAG_QUERY_REWRITE=true`, an off-topic question plus a
  transient 429 on the rewrite call gives HTTP 503 instead of "not found".
  - **Confidence:** confirmed by reading.
  - **Fix:** Catch `GenerationError` around `_rewrite` and the retry generation.
  Fall back to the first-round abstention and record the failure in the trace.
  - **Related, minor:** If the rewrite returns no usable queries, retrieval and
  generation are repeated on identical context. Skip the retry when `queries` is
  empty.

  **8. Dropping a collection races with in-flight writes and can bring it back**
  — `storage/collection.py:82-90`
  - **Defect:** `drop()` removes the cache entry and runs `rmtree` without
  taking `collection.lock`.
  - **Scenario:** An upload already running on the old `Collection` object calls
  `persist()`, which recreates the directory (`mkdir(parents=True)`) with the
  old data. The "dropped" collection comes back, and its contents differ from
  any newly opened handle.
  - **Confidence:** plausible (timing-dependent).
  - **Fix:** Acquire the collection's lock inside `drop()` and mark the handle
  as closed so that `persist()` refuses to write.

  **9. A replaced file can stay stale when its new content matches another
  document** — `ingestion_service.py:121-123`
  - **Defect:** The duplicate check runs before replace-by-name.
  - **Scenario:** Ingest `a.txt` (v1) and `b.txt` (v2), then edit `a.txt` so it
  equals v2. Re-ingesting `a.txt` reports `skipped_duplicate`, and the old v1
  content stays indexed and citable under `a.txt`.
  - **Confidence:** confirmed by reading.
  - **Fix:** When the content is a duplicate but `find_by_source(source)` points
  to a different `doc_id`, still remove the stale record for that name.

  **10. Upload memory is unbounded, and the docs wrongly say uploads never touch
  disk** — `interfaces/api.py:80-95`
  - **Memory:** Every accepted file (up to 50 MB each, and Starlette allows 1000
  files per form by default) is held in `payloads` until the whole batch is
  ingested.
  - **Disk:** The "bounded read" does not prevent buffering. Starlette has
  already spooled each part to a temporary file on disk once it exceeds 1 MB.
  That contradicts `01_ARCHITECTURE.md` §5 ("Uploads never touch disk outside
  the index").
  - **Confidence:** plausible (proportionate risk for a single user).
  - **Fix:** Cap the number of files and the total request size, for example
  with request-size middleware or a `max_files` limit. Correct the docs.

  **11. Model output can reach the logs despite content redaction** —
  `query_service.py:173`, logged at `:96`
  - **Defect:** A schema-validation failure embeds pydantic's `ValidationError`
  text, which includes `input_value=` (part of the model's answer, which can
  contain document text). `query_failed` then logs `error=str(exc)` at ERROR
  level regardless of `RAG_LOG_CONTENT`.
  - **Confidence:** confirmed by reading.
  - **Fix:** Log only the error count and locations
  (`exc.errors(include_input=False)`), or pass the message through
  `content_field`.

  **12. HTML-escaping passages can leak entities into answers** —
  `generation/prompts.py:83-84`
  - **Defect:** Passage text is sent with `html.escape`, so "AT&T" or "x < 5"
  reach the model as `AT&amp;T` and `x &lt; 5`. Answers or quotes may then
  contain `&amp;`.
  - **Confidence:** plausible (depends on the model).
  - **Fix:** Neutralise only the delimiter tags (for example replace `</source>`
  and `<source` case-insensitively) instead of escaping all text.
  Alternatively, unescape the answer afterwards.

  **13. Duplicate citation entries** — `generation/citations.py:27-31`
  - **Defect:** Ids are de-duplicated *before* `strip("[]")`. If the model lists
  `"[S1]"` in `citations` (the format it is told to use inline) and also writes
  `[S1]` in the answer, both resolve to S1.
  - **Scenario (reproduced):** This yields 2 `Citation` objects for S1.
  - **Confidence:** confirmed.
  - **Fix:** Normalise each id first (strip, remove brackets, upper-case), then
  de-duplicate.

  **14. Documentation that does not match the code**
  - `01_ARCHITECTURE.md` §7 on crash recovery: see #2.
  - §5 says a missing collection returns `CollectionEmpty`; the code raises
  `CollectionNotFoundError` (404).
  - The §4 flow diagram places the gate before the "LLM configured?" check and
  routes gate failure to rewrite. In code, no-LLM mode is decided first, and a
  gate failure there returns `abstained` with no rewrite.
  - "Per-file isolation" holds only for `RAGError`/`OSError` (#6).
  - "Uploads never touch disk" is false (#10).

  **15. Missing tests for important behaviour**
  - BM25 and hybrid results after an ingest or delete (cache invalidation, #5).
  - The rewrite retry triggered by the gate: only the path where the LLM says
  `answerable=false` is tested.
  - The rewrite call failing (#7).
  - Crash recovery for delete/replace, i.e. ghost records (#2).
  - A failed replace preserving the old version (#1).
  - Duplicate base names in `ingest_paths` (#3).
  - No chunk text being lost (#4). A property test would catch it: every
  non-whitespace token of the input appears in some chunk.
  - Concurrent query + ingest, and drop + ingest, through the API.

  Scratch reproduction scripts are in
  `C:/Users/rigu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/c--Users-rigu-Documents-RAG-project/1
  0590c60-1e42-40a6-9ed9-e4802186ca93/scratchpad/` (`chk2.py` for the chunker,
  `chk3.py` for #1 and #2, `chk4.py` for #3 and #13).

      59 +        """Append chunks shorter than ``min_chars`` to their pr
         +edecessor.                                                     
      60 +                                                               
      61 +        A short trailing line ("Contact: x@y.com") can end up a
         +lone when the previous                                         
      62 +        chunk leaves no overlap; dropping it would lose text, s
         +o it is kept with the                                          
      63 +        preceding chunk (a small overflow of ``chunk_size`` is 
         +accepted). Only a section                                      
      64 +        whose *entire* text is shorter than ``min_chars`` is dr
         +opped, in ``chunk``.                                           
      65 +        """                                                    
      66 +        result: list[str] = []                                 
      67 +        for chunk in chunks:                                   
      68 +            if result and len(chunk) < self.min_chars:         
      69 +                result[-1] = f"{result[-1]}\n{chunk}"          
      70 +            else:                                              
      71 +                result.append(chunk)                           
      72 +        return result

      61 +# Only the tags that structure the prompt are neutralised; all 
         +other text is passed                                           
      62 +# verbatim so "AT&T" or "x < 5" reach the model (and its quotes
         +) unaltered.                                                   
      63 +_STRUCTURAL_TAG = re.compile(r"<(/?\s*(?:sources?|question)\b)"
         +, re.IGNORECASE)                                               
      64 +                                                               
      65 +                                                               
      66 +def neutralise_tags(text: str) -> str:                         
      67 +    """Stop document or question text from opening/closing the 
         +prompt delimiters."""                                          
      68 +    return _STRUCTURAL_TAG.sub(r"&lt;\1", text)

         - §4.2) | Same message, reason e.g. "The sources do not mention 
         -the CEO." | `status=abstained attempts=1` |                    
      32 -| **Retrieval returns bad context** | Not detectable per query 
         -without labels; detected in aggregate by `rag eval` (hit@k) | R
         -untime: the LLM should abstain rather than guess. Optional `RAG
         -_QUERY_REWRITE` retries with rephrased queries. Offline: tune p
         -er 06 §5 | A safe "not found" rather than a wrong answer | `ret
         -rieved_chunk_ids`, `best_dense`, `rewrites` |                  
      33 -| **Ambiguous query** | The LLM sees partial or multiple readin
         -gs | Prompt: answer the supported part and say what is missing.
         - No clarification loop (single-shot) | Partial answer naming th
         -e missing piece | — |                                          
      34 -| **LLM API timeout** | `APITimeoutError` after SDK retries (`R
         -AG_LLM_MAX_RETRIES`, exponential backoff) | `GenerationError(re
         -tryable=True)` | HTTP **503** `{"error":"GenerationError",…,"re
         -tryable":true}` | `query_failed` (ERROR) with trace fields |   
      35 -| **Rate limiting (429)** | `RateLimitError` after SDK retries 
         -(honouring `retry-after`) | `GenerationError(retryable=True)` |
         - HTTP 503, `retryable: true` | `query_failed` |                
      36 -| **LLM 5xx / network failure** | `APIStatusError ≥ 500` / `API
         -ConnectionError` | `GenerationError(retryable=True)` | HTTP 503
         - | `query_failed` |                                            
      37 -| **Auth / request error (401/400)** | `AuthenticationError` / 
         -`BadRequestError` | `GenerationError(retryable=False)` with gui
         -dance ("set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY or use RAG_LLM_PROVIDER=none") | 
         -HTTP 503, `retryable: false` | `query_failed` |                
      38 -| **Model refusal** | `stop_reason == "refusal"` (after server-
         -side fallback, if enabled) | `GenerationError` | "the model dec
         -lined to answer this request" | `query_failed` |               
      39 -| **Truncated output** | `stop_reason == "max_tokens"` | `Gener
         -ationError` naming `RAG_LLM_MAX_TOKENS` | Error message | `quer
         -y_failed` |                                                    
      40 -| **Malformed output** | JSON decode or schema validation fails
         - | `GenerationError`; rare with structured outputs | Error; nev
         -er shown as an answer | `query_failed` |

      188  - **Trade-off:** Absorbing short tails can overflow `chunk_siz
           e` by up to
      189    `min_chunk_chars` plus a newline. We accept that small overf
           low so no text is lost.

      191 +                                                              
      192 +### 2026-09-30 — D13: Missing credentials must produce an acti
          +onable error (found by manual run)                            
      193 +- **Context:** Running `rag ask` on a machine with no `ANTHROP
          +IC_API_KEY` printed a                                         
      194 +  raw traceback. The SDK raises a bare `TypeError` ("Could not
          + resolve authentication                                       
      195 +  method") that isn't an `AnthropicError`, so none of the mapp

          +they never exercised the

          +sError` to

          +h an empty config                                             
      201 +  directory.

          +rrors. Only a manual                                          
      203 +  end-to-end run exposes the errors we didn't know about. This
          + is the most likely

        1  # RAG Generator
        2 +                                                              
        3 +Hand it **any set of documents at runtime** (PDF, DOCX, Markdo
          +wn, text) and ask                                             
        4 +questions. Answers are grounded in those documents, with check
          +able citations (file,                                         
        5 +page, passage), and the system says "not found" instead of gue
          +ssing. Different                                              
        6 +document sets live in separate **collections**, so no code cha
          +nges are needed.                                              
        7 +                                                              
        8 +> Built for an agentic-coding assessment. The design reasoning
          + is in [`docs/`](docs/),                                      
        9 +> and the chronological record of decisions, including three d
          +efaults that                                                  
       10 +> measurement overturned, is in [`docs/DECISION_LOG.md`](docs/
          +DECISION_LOG.md).                                             
       11 +                                                              
       12 +## Architecture                                               
       13 +                                                              
       14 +```mermaid                                                    
       15 +flowchart LR                                                  
       16 +    U[CLI / REST API] --> I[IngestionService]                 
       17 +    U --> Q[QueryService]                                     
       18 +    I --> P[Parse<br/>PyMuPDF · docx · text] --> C[Chunk<br/>r
          +ecursive, page-bounded] --> E[Embed<br/>local bge-small ONNX] 
          +--> S[(Collection<br/>NumPy index + catalog)]                 
       19 +    Q --> R[Retrieve<br/>dense · bm25 · hybrid] --> S         
       20 +    R --> RR[Rerank<br/>off by default] --> G{Relevance<br/>ga
          +te}                                                           
       21 +    G -- off-topic --> A0[Abstain, no LLM call]               
       22 +    G -- ok --> L[Claude<br/>structured JSON] --> V[Validate c
          +itations] --> A[Answer + citations + trace]                   
       23 +    L -. "answerable=false<br/>(optional one retry)" .-> W[Rew
          +rite query] --> R                                             
       24 +```                                                           
       25 +                                                              
       26 +- **Two independent paths.** Ingestion writes to a per-collect
          +ion index. Queries only                                       
       27 +  read from it.                                               
       28 +- **Every component sits behind a small protocol:** `DocumentP
          +arser`,                                                       
       29 +  `EmbeddingProvider`, `VectorStore`, `Retriever`, `Reranker` 
          +and `LLMProvider`. The                                        
       30 +  only module that maps configuration to vendors is `orchestra
          +tion/factory.py`                                              
       31 +  ([05_API_AND_COMPONENT_DESIGN.md](docs/05_API_AND_COMPONENT_
          +DESIGN.md)).                                                  
       32 +- **Grounding:**                                              
       33 +  - Passages are shown to the model as `[S1]…[Sk]`.           
       34 +  - The model must cite them, or return `answerable=false`.   
       35 +  - Citations it wasn't given are dropped.                    
       36 +  - Every answer carries a `grounding_status`: `grounded`, `un
          +verified`, `abstained`                                        
       37 +    or `retrieval_only`.                                      
       38 +                                                              
       39 +## Tech stack                                                 
       40 +                                                              
       41 +| Concern | Choice | Why (short version; details in [02](docs/
          +02_ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md) and [03](docs/03_TRADEOFF_ANALYS
          +IS.md)) |                                                     
       42 +|---------|--------|------|                                   
       43 +| Parsing | PyMuPDF, python-docx | Exact page numbers for cita
          +tions. Pip-only install |                                     
       44 +| Chunking | Own recursive splitter (700 / 140 characters) | C
          +orpus-agnostic. Never crosses pages. Size chosen by measuremen
          +t |                                                           
       45 +| Embeddings | fastembed `BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5` (local ONNX)
          + | No key, documents stay local, no PyTorch |                 
       46 +| Vector store | NumPy exact search, one atomic `index.npz` pe
          +r collection | Zero infrastructure at this scale. pgvector or 
          +FAISS is a one-class swap |                                   
       47 +| Retrieval | Dense (BM25 and hybrid RRF available) | Dense me
          +asured best on the sample corpus |                            
       48 +| Reranking | Cross-encoder available, **off** | Measured: no 
          +hit@5 gain, +1.5 s per query |                                
       49 +| LLM | Claude `claude-opus-5-5` at effort `low`, via the Anth
          +ropic SDK | Best cite-or-abstain discipline. Any model via `RA
          +G_LLM_MODEL` |                                                
       50 +| Orchestration | Plain Python | One linear pipeline plus one 
          +conditional retry. No framework needed |                      
       51 +| Config | pydantic-settings, `.env` | Typed, validated, nothi
          +ng hard-coded |                                               
       52 +| Interfaces | typer CLI, FastAPI (Swagger UI at `/docs`) | Ev
          +aluator-friendly with no front-end code |                     
       53 +                                                              
       54 +## Quick start                                                
       55 +                                                              
       56 +Requires Python ≥ 3.11. Tested on 3.14 / Windows 11.          
       57 +                                                              
       58 +```bash                                                       
       59 +git clone <repo> && cd rag-generator                          
       60 +uv sync --extra dev             # or: python -m venv .venv && 
          +pip install -e ".[dev]"                                       
       61 +source .venv/bin/activate       # Windows: .venv\Scripts\activ
          +ate                                                           
       62 +cp .env.example .env            # optional: add ANTHROPIC_API_
          +KEY for generated answers                                     
       63 +```                                                           
       64 +                                                              
       65 +The first ingest downloads the embedding model once (about 130
          + MB).                                                         
       66 +                                                              
       67 +### 1. Ingest a document set                                  
       68 +                                                              
       69 +```bash                                                       
       70 +rag ingest data/sample/northwind -c northwind                 
       71 +```                                                           
       72 +                                                              
       73 +```text                                                       
       74 +  [ingested] benefits_overview.md: 3 chunks                   
       75 +  [ingested] employee_handbook.md: 7 chunks                   
       76 +  [ingested] firmware_release_notes.docx: 2 chunks            
       77 +  [ingested] it_security_policy.txt: 5 chunks                 
       78 +  [ingested] nw200_operator_manual.pdf: 7 chunks              
       79 +  [ingested] warehouse_safety_procedures.md: 4 chunks         
       80 +Collection 'northwind': 6 ingested, 0 failed (5.3s)           
       81 +```                                                           
       82 +                                                              
       83 +### 2. Ask a question                                         
       84 +                                                              
       85 +With `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set, you get a generated answer with 
          +citations:                                                    
       86 +                                                              
       87 +```bash                                                       
       88 +rag ask "What does error E-221 mean and what should I do?" -c 
          +northwind --trace                                             
       89 +```                                                           
       90 +                                                              
       91 +The output is the answer text with inline `[S1]` markers, `sta
          +tus: grounded`, a                                             
       92 +`Sources:` list (`[S1] nw200_operator_manual.pdf, p.3 — "…"`),
          + and the trace (stage                                         
       93 +timings, tokens, retrieved chunk IDs).                        
       94 +                                                              
       95 +Without a key, use `--no-llm` for **retrieval-only** mode. Thi
          +s is real output:                                             
       96 +                                                              
       97 +```text                                                       
       98 +$ rag ask "What does error E-221 mean?" -c northwind --no-llm 
          +-k 2                                                          
       99 +status: retrieval_only                                        
      100 +                                                              
      101 +Top passages (retrieval-only mode):                           
      102 +  1. nw200_operator_manual.pdf, p.3 (score 0.700)             
      103 +     Error codes When the status light turns amber or red, the
          + Fleet Manager console shows an error code. ...               
      104 +  2. nw200_operator_manual.pdf, p.3 (score 0.603)             
      105 +     E-502 Localisation lost: the robot cannot match its posit
          +ion to the site map. ...                                      
      106 +                                                              
      107 +$ rag ask "How do I bake sourdough bread?" -c northwind --no-l
          +lm                                                            
      108 +status: abstained                                             
      109 +reason: No passage was relevant enough (best similarity 0.47 <
          + threshold 0.50).                                             
      110 +```                                                           
      111 +                                                              
      112 +### 3. A completely different document set, with no code chang
          +e                                                             
      113 +                                                              
      114 +```bash                                                       
      115 +rag ingest data/sample/gardening -c garden                    
      116 +rag ask "What can't go in the compost?" -c garden             
      117 +rag collections          # garden, northwind                  
      118 +```                                                           
      119 +                                                              
      120 +### 4. The REST API                                           
      121 +                                                              
      122 +```bash                                                       
      123 +rag serve                # http://127.0.0.1:8000/docs  (upload
          + and query from the browser)                                  
      124 +curl -F "files=@my.pdf" -F "files=@notes.docx" localhost:8000/
          +collections/mine/documents                                    
      125 +curl -X POST localhost:8000/collections/mine/query -H 'content
          +-type: application/json' \                                    
      126 +     -d '{"question": "What is the refund policy?", "include_p
          +assages": true}'                                              
      127 +```                                                           
      128 +                                                              
      129 +Other commands: `rag docs -c NAME`, `rag delete DOC_ID -c NAME
          +`, `rag drop -c NAME`.                                        
      130 +                                                              
      131 +## Configuration                                              
      132 +                                                              
      133 +Everything is set by environment variables or `.env` (preceden
          +ce: CLI flag > env >                                          
      134 +`.env` > default). The full list with comments is in [`.env.ex
          +ample`](.env.example).                                        
      135 +The most useful ones:                                         
      136 +                                                              
      137 +| Variable | Default | Purpose |                              
      138 +|----------|---------|---------|                              
      139 +| `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` | — | Enables generation. Without it, us
          +e `--no-llm` / `RAG_LLM_PROVIDER=none` |                      
      140 +| `RAG_LLM_MODEL`, `RAG_LLM_EFFORT` | `claude-opus-5-5`, `low`
          + | e.g. `claude-sonnet-5-5` or `claude-haiku-4-5` for lower co
          +st |                                                          
      141 +| `RAG_RETRIEVAL_MODE` | `dense` | `bm25` or `hybrid` for iden
          +tifier-heavy corpora |                                        
      142 +| `RAG_TOP_K` | `5` | Passages given to the LLM |             
      143 +| `RAG_CHUNK_SIZE`, `RAG_CHUNK_OVERLAP` | `700`, `140` | Chara
          +cters |                                                       
      144 +| `RAG_MIN_RELEVANCE` | `0.50` | Off-topic gate. **Recalibrate
          + with `rag eval` if you change the embedding model** |        
      145 +| `RAG_RERANKER` | `none` | `cross_encoder` enables local Mini
          +LM reranking |                                                
      146 +| `RAG_QUERY_REWRITE` | `false` | One bounded corrective retry
          + when context is insufficient |                               
      147 +| `RAG_EMBEDDING_MODEL` | `BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5` | Any faste
          +mbed model. Changing it requires re-ingestion (the index refus
          +es mixed vectors) |                                           
      148 +| `RAG_DATA_DIR` | `.rag_data` | Where collections are stored 
          +|                                                             
      149 +| `RAG_LOG_FORMAT`, `RAG_LOG_CONTENT` | `text`, `false` | Use 
          +`json` for machine logs. Document text is redacted unless `RAG
          +_LOG_CONTENT=true` |                                          
      150 +                                                              
      151 +Invalid configuration fails at start-up with a message naming 
          +the variable.                                                 
      152 +                                                              
      153 +## Testing                                                    
      154 +                                                              
      155 +```bash                                                       
      156 +pytest                   # 147 tests, offline, deterministic, 
          +no key, no model download (~10 s)                             
      157 +pytest -m slow           # + real-model retrieval regression w
          +ith bge-small                                                 
      158 +ruff check src tests scripts                                  
      159 +```                                                           
      160 +                                                              
      161 +The offline suite uses a deterministic hashing embedder and a 
          +scripted fake LLM. It                                         
      162 +covers every failure mode in [08](docs/08_FAILURE_MODES.md): c
          +orrupt, empty, scanned                                        
      163 +and unsupported files; duplicates; model mismatch; LLM timeout
          +s and rate limits;                                            
      164 +invalid citations; crash recovery; prompt-delimiter injection.
          + See                                                          
      165 +[07_TESTING.md](docs/07_TESTING.md).                          
      166 +                                                              
      167 +## Evaluation                                                 
      168 +                                                              
      169 +```bash                                                       
      170 +rag eval data/eval/northwind_eval.jsonl -c northwind          
          +  # retrieval: dense, bm25, hybrid                            
      171 +rag eval data/eval/northwind_eval.jsonl -c northwind --answers
          + --judge   # + answer quality (needs key)                     
      172 +python scripts/retrieval_sweep.py --rerank                    
          +  # chunk size × mode × reranker                              
      173 +```                                                           
      174 +                                                              
      175 +The measured retrieval results (26 questions, 6 documents) for
          + the shipped                                                  
      176 +configuration are hit@1 0.905, hit@5 1.000 and MRR 0.933. Eval
          +uation changed three                                          
      177 +design-time defaults:                                         
      178 +                                                              
      179 +1. **Hybrid → dense.** BM25 missed a paraphrase, and equal-wei
          +ght RRF then demoted                                          
      180 +   the correct dense hit.                                     
      181 +2. **Chunk size 1000 → 700.**                                 
      182 +3. **Relevance gate lowered to 0.50.** On-topic unanswerable q
          +uestions score as high                                        
      183 +   as answerable ones, so the gate can only filter off-topic q
          +uestions, and the LLM                                         
      184 +   must handle the rest.                                      
      185 +                                                              
      186 +The full analysis, and how each metric maps to an architectura
          +l fix, is in                                                  
      187 +[06_EVALUATION.md](docs/06_EVALUATION.md). *Answer-quality met
          +rics haven't been run                                         
      188 +yet*, because no API key was available during development. The
          + harness is                                                   
      189 +implemented and tested.                                       
      190 +                                                              
      191 +## Documentation                                              
      192 +                                                              
      193 +| Doc | Contents |                                            
      194 +|-----|----------|                                            
      195 +| [00 Requirements](docs/00_REQUIREMENTS.md) | Source vs spec 
          +vs assumption vs decision; acceptance criteria |

          +ws, lifecycle, persistence, failure paths |

      201 +| [06 Evaluation](docs/06_EVALUATION.md) · [07 Testing](docs/0

          +ility](docs/10_OBSERVABILITY.md) | |                          
      202 +| [Decision log](docs/DECISION_LOG.md) | D1–D13, including rev
          +isions driven by measurement and by code review |             
      203 +                                                              
      204 +## Limitations                                                
      205 +                                                              
      206 +- No OCR: scanned PDFs are detected and rejected with a clear 
          +message. Tables are                                           
      207 +  flattened to text.                                          
      208 +- Single process and single user. There is no authentication, 
          +so don't expose                                               
      209 +  `rag serve` publicly. The in-memory index suits collections 
          +up to about 10⁵ chunks.                                       
      210 +- Single-shot Q&A: there is no conversation memory.           
      211 +- Grounding is verified at the level of citation IDs at runtim
          +e. Claim-level                                                
      212 +  faithfulness is measured offline (`--judge`), not enforced p
          +er answer.                                                    
      213 +- The evaluation set is small (26 questions): its results are 
          +directional.                                                  
      214 +- Chunk sizing uses characters as a proxy for tokens, which is
          + less precise for                                             
      215 +  non-English text.                                           
      216 +                                                              
      217 +## Future improvements                                        
      218 +                                                              
      219 +- Run the answer-quality evaluation against Claude and tune th
          +e prompt from the                                             
      220 +  measured false-abstention and false-answer rates.           
      221 +- BM25 stemming and weighted RRF, then re-test hybrid on an id
          +entifier-heavy corpus.                                        
      222 +- OCR parser (Tesseract or Docling) behind the existing `Docum
          +entParser` interface.                                         
      223 +- A pgvector store for multi-user deployment, plus authenticat
          +ion and per-tenant                                            
      224 +  collections.                                                
      225 +- Conversation memory with follow-up question condensing.     
      226 +- OpenTelemetry spans (which map one-to-one onto `StageTimer` 
          +stages) and a metrics                                         
      227 +  endpoint.                                                   
      228 +

          +transcripts are                                               
      232 +provided separately with the submission, as the brief requires
          +.

● I built the RAG generator, and it's committed with a clean working tree. You
  can ingest any document set at runtime and ask questions; answers cite file,
  page and passage, and the system says "not found" instead of guessing. The
  offline suite (147 tests) and the 2 real-model tests pass, and lint is clean.

  What hasn't been verified: no API key was available, so I've never seen Claude
  actually generate an answer. Generation is tested only against a fake model,
  and the answer-quality metrics haven't been run. Setting ANTHROPIC_API_KEY and
  running rag eval data/eval/northwind_eval.jsonl -c northwind --answers
  --judge would produce them (about $0.50).

  Two things to check before you submit:
  - Commit authorship. I authored my two commits as "Akash Kulthe
    akashgajanan.kulthe@joveo.com". The repo also contains your commit 92c2171
    by "Rishabh R Gupta rigu@ciklum.com". If one identity is wrong, fix it
    before submitting; I haven't rewritten any history.
  - Transcripts. The brief requires complete AI-agent transcripts. The README
    says they're provided separately, so export this session and include it.

  No candidate brief was attached. I used the summary in your message as the
  source brief, recorded verbatim in docs/BRIEF.md. Requirements are tagged by
  origin, so your own engineering spec isn't passed off as assessment
  requirements.

  Architecture

  Ingestion writes to a per-collection index; queries only read from it:
  - Ingestion: parse → chunk → embed → store.
  - Query: retrieve → (optional rerank) → relevance gate → Claude → citation
    check → answer.
  - Swappable parts: each component sits behind a small interface, and only
    orchestration/factory.py picks concrete implementations.
  - Agentic behaviour: there is exactly one piece, off by default: a single
    rewrite-and-retry when the context looks insufficient.

  Major decisions and trade-offs

  ┌───────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Component   │        Choice        │        Trade-off accepted        │
  ├───────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │               │ PyMuPDF,             │ No OCR (scanned PDFs are         │
  │ Parsing       │ python-docx, plain   │ detected and rejected); tables   │
  │               │ text                 │ flattened to text                │
  ├───────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │               │                      │ Lower quality ceiling than       │
  │ Embeddings    │ Local bge-small      │ hosted models, in exchange for   │
  │               │ model                │ no key and documents staying     │
  │               │                      │ local                            │
  ├───────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │               │                      │ Single process, index held in    │
  │ Vector store  │ NumPy exact search   │ RAM; pgvector would be the swap  │
  │               │                      │ for multi-user                   │
  ├───────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │               │                      │ About 150 lines of glue in       │
  │ Orchestration │ Plain Python         │ exchange for fully visible       │
  │               │                      │ prompts and control flow         │
  ├───────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │               │ Numbered source IDs, │ Proves which passage was cited,  │
  │ Citations     │  checked after       │ not that it supports the claim;  │
  │               │ generation           │ that is measured offline         │
  └───────────────┴──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

  What the evidence changed

  The retrieval sweep (26 questions, CPU only, no key needed) overturned three
  design-time defaults:
  - Hybrid → dense retrieval. BM25 has no stemming and missed a paraphrase
    ("recharge" vs "charging"). Equal-weight fusion then pushed the correct
    dense result out of the top 10.
  - Chunk size 1000 → 700. Better ranking with shorter passages.
  - Relevance gate set to 0.50. On-topic unanswerable questions ("CEO?", "list
    price?") score as high as answerable ones, so a similarity threshold can
    only filter clearly off-topic questions. The model has to catch the rest.
  - Reranker left off. It costs about 1.5 s per query, and the right passage was
    already in the top 5 for every question.

  The sample is small (21 answerable questions), so these results are
  directional.

  Bugs found after the first build:
  - Code review: 15 issues, all fixed with regression tests. Among them: a
    failed replace deleting the old version, files with the same name silently
    overwriting each other, and the chunker dropping short trailing lines.
  - Manual run: with no key, the SDK raised an uncaught error that printed a raw
    traceback. It now fails with an actionable message.

  How to run and test

  uv sync --extra dev
  rag ingest data/sample/northwind -c northwind
  rag ask "What does E-221 mean?" -c northwind --no-llm     # drop --no-llm once
  ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is set
  rag ingest data/sample/gardening -c garden                 # a second document
  set, no code change
  rag serve                                                  # browser
  upload/query at http://127.0.0.1:8000/docs
  pytest && pytest -m slow

  Limitations and future work

  - Limitations: no authentication, so don't expose rag serve publicly;
    single-shot Q&A with no conversation memory; a small evaluation set.
  - Future work: run the answer-quality evaluation, add stemming and weighted
    fusion to hybrid retrieval, an OCR parser, pgvector plus authentication, and
    tracing.

  Files

  - Docs: 00–10, BRIEF.md and DECISION_LOG.md (13 dated entries, including the
    revisions) are in docs/.
  - Code and tests: 69 files in src/ and tests/.
  - Scripts: scripts/retrieval_sweep.py and scripts/build_sample_data.py.
  - Data: two sample document sets, plus the evaluation set in data/eval/.
  - Config: pyproject.toml, uv.lock, .env.example, .gitignore, .gitattributes.

  Assessment readiness

  - [x] A completely different document set works without code changes (tested)
  - [x] Embedder, vector store and LLM are replaceable
  - [x] Every answer has citations and a trust status; "not found" is handled
    explicitly
  - [x] Retrieval quality is measurable offline, with measured results
  - [ ] Answer faithfulness measured against Claude (harness ready; needs a key)
  - [ ] AI-agent transcripts exported and included

      287  
      288  ---
      289  
      290 +## ADR-014 — OpenAI as a second provider for generation and (o
          +pt-in) embeddings                                             
      291 +                                                              
      292 +**Status:** Accepted                                          
      293 +                                                              
      294 +**Context.** Users may already hold an OpenAI key rather than 
          +an Anthropic one, or                                          
      295 +need a model hosted on Azure OpenAI or an OpenAI-compatible se
          +rver (Ollama, vLLM).                                          
      296 +The `LLMProvider` and `EmbeddingProvider` interfaces were desi
          +gned for exactly this                                         
      297 +swap. This ADR records how the second vendor was added.       
      298 +                                                              
      299 +**Decision.**                                                 
      300 +- `RAG_LLM_PROVIDER=openai` → `OpenAIProvider` (official `open
          +ai` SDK). It uses Chat                                        
      301 +  Completions **strict JSON-schema structured outputs**, so th
          +e same prompts, answer                                        
      302 +  schema, citation validation and abstention logic run unchang
          +ed. Our schemas already                                       
      303 +  meet strict mode's rules (`additionalProperties: false`, eve
          +ry property required),                                        
      304 +  and a test asserts this.                                    
      305 +- `RAG_EMBEDDING_PROVIDER=openai` → `OpenAIEmbeddingProvider` 
      306 +  (`text-embedding-3-small`). This is **opt-in**: it sends doc
          +ument text to OpenAI at                                       
      307 +  ingest time, which reverses ADR-003's privacy property.     
      308 +- Model names default **per provider** (`claude-opus-5-5` / `g
          +pt-5.5`;                                                      
      309 +  `BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5` / `text-embedding-3-small`) when `R
          +AG_LLM_MODEL` /                                               
      310 +  `RAG_EMBEDDING_MODEL` are unset. `RAG_OPENAI_BASE_URL` enabl
          +es Azure and compatible                                       
      311 +  servers.                                                    
      312 +- `reasoning_effort` and `temperature` are sent only when conf
          +igured, because each is                                       
      313 +  rejected by one family of models (reasoning vs non-reasoning
          +).                                                            
      314 +- The `openai` package is an optional extra (`.[openai]`). The
          + factory imports it                                           
      315 +  lazily and gives an install hint if it is missing.          
      316 +                                                              
      317 +**Alternatives.**                                             
      318 +- *OpenAI Responses API* instead of Chat Completions: a newer 
          +surface, but Chat                                             
      319 +  Completions is what OpenAI-compatible servers implement, so 
          +one code path covers                                          
      320 +  OpenAI, Azure and Ollama.                                   
      321 +- *A generic multi-vendor layer (LiteLLM, LangChain)*: many ve
          +ndors for free, but it                                        
      322 +  adds a large dependency and hides request details (structure
          +d-output support varies                                       
      323 +  by backend). Two thin providers are about 120 lines each.   
      324 +- *Tool calling to force JSON*: works on older models, but str
          +ict structured outputs                                        
      325 +  is the direct mechanism.                                    
      326 +                                                              
     … 22 more lines (ctrl+o to expand)

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● OpenAI now works as a second provider: set OPENAI_API_KEY and
  RAG_LLM_PROVIDER=openai, and Claude remains the default. It's committed, and
  all 181 offline tests plus the 2 real-model tests pass. I haven't seen a real
  OpenAI answer, though, because there was no key here. Every OpenAI response
  was faked in the tests, and the only real run was the no-key case.

  uv sync --extra dev --extra openai        # or: pip install -e ".[dev,openai]"
  export OPENAI_API_KEY=sk-...
  export RAG_LLM_PROVIDER=openai            # model defaults to gpt-5.5;
  override with RAG_LLM_MODEL
  rag ask "What does error E-221 mean?" -c northwind

  What was added:
  - Answers from OpenAI. OpenAI returns the same structured answer Claude does,
    so citation checking and "not found" handling work unchanged; the whole
    query path is tested through the OpenAI provider with a fake client.
  - Optional OpenAI embeddings (RAG_EMBEDDING_PROVIDER=openai,
    text-embedding-3-small). This is opt-in because it sends document text to
    OpenAI when you ingest. Switching also means re-ingesting existing
    collections.
  - Other endpoints. RAG_OPENAI_BASE_URL points the same provider at Azure
    OpenAI or a compatible server such as Ollama.
  - Model names default per provider when you don't set them: claude-opus-5-5 /
    gpt-5.5, and bge-small / text-embedding-3-small.

  Things to know:
  - The off-topic filter is off with OpenAI embeddings. Its 0.50 cutoff was only
    measured for the local model, and OpenAI similarity scores run on a
    different scale. So it stays off, with a start-up warning, until you run rag
    eval, which suggests a value to set. Reusing 0.50 could have silently
    rejected questions the documents can answer.
  - gpt-5.5 is an unverified default. I picked it from the SDK's model list and
    couldn't confirm access, price or answer quality. Running rag eval --answers
    with your key would settle that; RAG_LLM_MODEL changes it.

  Fixed along the way: .env.example had the Claude model name written in. Anyone
  copying it and switching to OpenAI would have sent claude-opus-5-5 to OpenAI.
  Those lines are now commented out so the per-provider defaults apply, and I
  checked that a copied file resolves correctly with OpenAI.

  The reasoning is written up in docs/02_ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md (ADR-014) and
  docs/DECISION_LOG.md (D14). The README and the design, testing, failure-mode
  and security docs are updated to match.

  features developed so far

  you may have specified, and for what changed on disk since my last commit,
  before choosing anything.

          +all -e ".[ui]" (or uv sync --extra ui)'))                     
      225 +    app_path = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "ui" /

      238 +                                                              
      239 +def _start_api(api_url: str):                                 
      240 +    """Start `rag serve` for --with-api and wait until /health
          + answers."""                                                  
      241 +    import subprocess                                         
      242 +    import sys                                                
      243 +    import time                                               
      244 +    from urllib.error import URLError                         
      245 +    from urllib.parse import urlparse                         
      246 +    from urllib.request import urlopen                        
      247 +                                                              
      248 +    parsed = urlparse(api_url)                                
      249 +    command = [sys.executable, "-m", "rag_generator.interfaces
          +.cli", "serve",                                               
      250 +               "--host", parsed.hostname or "127.0.0.1", "--po
          +rt", str(parsed.port or 8000)]                                
      251 +    process = subprocess.Popen(command)                       
      252 +    typer.echo(f"Starting the API at {api_url} (the first star
          +t loads the embedding model)…")                               
      253 +    deadline = time.monotonic() + 120                         
      254 +    while time.monotonic() < deadline:                        
      255 +        if process.poll() is not None:                        
      256 +            raise _fail(RAGError("the API process exited; see 
          +its output above"))

      493  
      494  ---
      495  
      496 -## ADR-012 — Interfaces: CLI + REST API, no custom UI         
      496 +## ADR-012 — Interfaces: CLI + REST API, plus a Streamlit UI o
          +ver the API                                                   
      497  
      498 -**Status:** Accepted                                          
      498 +**Status:** Revised. The original decision was CLI + REST API 
          +with no custom UI. A                                          
      499 +Streamlit UI was added on request (DECISION_LOG D15); see "Rev
          +ision" below.                                                 
      500  
      501  **Context.** Users must supply documents at runtime and ask qu
           estions. Evaluators need
      502  a fast way to try the system.
     ...
      514  Both interfaces are thin adapters over the same services, so a
           dding a UI later is
      515  additive.
      516  
      517 +**Revision: Streamlit UI (`rag ui`).** The UI is a **pure HTTP
          + client** of the REST                                         
      518 +API. It imports no backend code: `rag_generator/ui/client.py` 
          +is its only way in.                                           
      519 +- *Why over REST rather than in-process:* one backend process 
          +serves the CLI, Swagger,                                      
      520 +  the UI and any other client. The UI can run on another machi
          +ne. And the API                                               
      521 +  contract (`/config`, `/evaluate`, the error bodies) is exerc
          +ised by a real client.                                        
      522 +- *What it cost:* three API additions, useful beyond the UI:  
      523 +  - `GET /config`: supported file types, limits, active models
          + and defaults, so the                                         
      524 +    UI hard-codes none of them.                               
      525 +  - `POST /collections/{c}/evaluate`: the evaluation harness o
          +ver HTTP.                                                     
      526 +  - A per-request `rewrite` flag on `/query`.                 
      527 +  - Also, the CLI and API now share one `run_evaluation()` ins
          +tead of duplicating it.                                       
      528 +- *Alternatives:*                                             
      529 +  - Streamlit in-process: one process, but it couples the UI t
          +o the backend's Python                                        
      530 +    environment and models.                                   
      531 +  - Gradio: similar, with less layout control for the evaluati
          +on dashboard.                                                 
      532 +  - A React SPA: the most polished, but a second toolchain.   
      533 +- *Trade-off accepted:* two processes to run. `rag ui --with-a
          +pi` starts both.                                              
      534 +  Like the API, the UI has **no authentication**, so it binds 
          +to 127.0.0.1 by default.                                      
      535 +                                                              
      536  ---
      537  
      538  ## ADR-013 — Evaluation: offline labelled dataset, retrieval m

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The Streamlit UI is built and talks to the backend only over the REST API. All
  225 offline tests pass and lint is clean. I checked it against a live server:
  rag serve with the real embedding model and the sample documents, driven by
  the real UI over HTTP.

  uv sync --extra ui          # or: pip install -e ".[ui]"
  rag ui --with-api           # starts the API and the UI →
  http://localhost:8501

  rag ui --api-url <url> connects to an API that's already running.

  What's in it:
  - Ask: chat with a collection. Each answer shows a status badge (grounded /
    unverified / not found / retrieval only), inline [S1] citation markers and
    source cards (file, page, snippet). It also shows the retrieved passages and
    a trace: timings, tokens, and similarity against the relevance cutoff. You
    can choose retrieval mode, top-k, retrieval-only and the rewrite retry per
    question.
  - Documents: upload files, see a per-file result, list documents, delete them,
    or drop the whole collection (after a confirmation checkbox). Accepted file
    types and limits come from the server. It already picked up the
    CSV/XLSX/PPTX support from the parallel session with no UI change.
  - Evaluate: upload a JSONL question set, compare retrieval modes and
    rerankers, see the best configuration and a suggested relevance cutoff,
    optionally score answers, and download the report.
  - System: the active models and server defaults.

  Backend changes the UI needed: a new GET /config (supported types, limits and
  models, never keys), a new POST /collections/{c}/evaluate, and a per-question
  rewrite option on /query. The CLI and the API now share one evaluation
  function. Unknown retrieval-mode names are now rejected; rag eval used to fall
  back to hybrid silently on a typo.

  Fixed while building it:
  - The server reported "no LLM" when one was injected without matching
    settings, which switched off the UI's answer mode.
  - Evaluation reports could contain NaN, which is invalid JSON. Undefined
    metrics are now null.
  - Streamlit listens on all network interfaces by default. rag ui binds to
    127.0.0.1, since the UI can delete data and has no login.
  - The no-API-key error now tells browser users to use the "Retrieval only"
    toggle instead of a CLI flag.

  One mistake of mine, now fixed: a doc-editing script emptied
  docs/05_API_AND_COMPONENT_DESIGN.md partway through. I restored it from the
  last commit (it had no uncommitted changes) and re-applied my edits; no other
  doc was affected.

  Answer generation wasn't tried for real. There was no API key, so the answer
  path was checked only for its error message, and the UI tests use a scripted
  model.

  Not committed. Another session is working in this repo at the same time (the
  CSV/XLSX/PPTX parsers). pyproject.toml, uv.lock, the README and several docs
  now mix my UI changes with that work. My own new files are
  src/rag_generator/ui/ and four test files; my other changes are in api.py,
  cli.py, factory.py, the evaluation modules and the docs. Should I commit
  everything together, or just the UI parts and leave the parser work to be
  committed separately?

● That 404 comes from opening http://127.0.0.1:8000/ in a browser. Port 8000 is
  the REST API, which has no page at /. The web UI runs separately on port 8501,
  and the API's interactive docs are at /docs. Nothing is broken, but a bare
  404 is a confusing first impression, so I'll make / redirect to the API docs.

● Nothing is broken. Your log shows the API running fine (GET /health returned
  200). The 404 was from opening the API's base address in a browser, and the
  API had no page there.

  There are three addresses:

  ┌────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │            URL             │                 What it is                  │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                            │ The web UI. Started with rag ui, or rag ui  │
  │ http://localhost:8501      │ --with-api to start both the API and the    │
  │                            │ UI.                                         │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ http://127.0.0.1:8000/docs │ The API's interactive docs (Swagger), which │
  │                            │  let you try every endpoint in the browser. │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ http://127.0.0.1:8000/     │ The bare API base address, which is what    │
  │                            │ you opened.                                 │
  └────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  You've already started rag serve, so run rag ui in a second terminal and open
  http://localhost:8501. If you get a Streamlit import error, install the UI
  extra first with uv sync --extra ui (or pip install -e ".[ui]").

  To make this less confusing, http://127.0.0.1:8000/ now redirects to /docs
  instead of returning a 404. I added a test for it, and the end-to-end tests
  and lint pass. Restart rag serve to pick it up. The /favicon.ico 404 is just
  the browser asking for a tab icon and is harmless.

● Found the bug: since api.py uses from __future__ import annotations, FastAPI
  resolves type hints by name from module globals, but my ClientLLM alias was
  defined inside create_app, so it couldn't be seen and override was treated as
  a required query parameter. I'll move the dependency to module level and
  access the app via request.app.state instead.
